===== .changeset/r5a-drafting-at-plan.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor

Drafting at `plan` (R5a, A-R5a-01 to A-R5a-06). A run admitted with `'draft'` for its task graph seats a planner at `plan`, under the role `planner`, with a critic under `plan-critic` and the rubric `plan.md`. The planner reads the locked spec and the repository, never the intent, and writes `.drafts/<run id>/graph.json`: build tasks only, each stating its `objective`. The runtime adds one review task over all of them, and the draft goes through `plan`'s gate under the drafted floor. The approved bytes are locked before any task exists.

- core: `DraftStation` and `AgentStation` gain `plan`, with rows in `STATION_CONTRACTS`, `CRITIC_CONTRACTS`, `DRAFT_ROLES`, `DRAFT_STATIONS` and `SEAT_TASK_IDS`. `Task` gains `objective: string | null`. `PolicyDocument` gains `planning?: { maxBuildTasks, roles }` and the resolved `Policy.planning` is that or `null`; validation refuses zero tasks, an empty or repeated role list, a role with no scope at `build`, and a reviewing role. A rejection at `plan` carries notes, as at `spec`.
- vault: `AdmissionRecord.artifacts.taskGraph` is `{ source: 'supplied', file }` or `{ source: 'drafted', bound }`. A record written before this holds the bare file and is read as supplied. Draft, critic, lock and rubric records accept `plan`.
- api: `RequestedArtifacts.taskGraph` takes `'draft'`, refused under a policy with no `planning`. Admission reads the bound instead of a graph: every allowed role must be compiled, scoped at `build`, and admitted for the trigger, and the worst-case cost is the bound's, which is the figure approval is compared with. `DraftingDrivers` gains `planner` and `planCritic`, and the host's endpoint seats gain both. A builder is given its own task's objective and those of the tasks it depends on. The report shows each drafted task's objective. `parseDraftedGraph`, `boundGraph`, `graphDraftPath`, `taskGraphGrant` and `OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES` are exported.
- cli: `create --draft-plan`, in place of `--graph`. `draft <run id> plan` prints the drafted graph.
- compiler: the roles `planner` and `plan-critic` ship beside the others.
- conformance: sixteen entries in `registry/planning.ts`, each shown failing with what it guards removed.

@@ -273,6 +273,28 @@ role's `budget` is per task, and `maxTokens` counts the tokens a model reads
 from its cache: a model reading the repository spends most of its tokens
 there. The example gives each drafter 400000 and every other seat 200000.
 
+A run can draft its task graph too: `create --draft-plan` in place of
+`--graph`. A planner then reads the locked spec and the repository at `plan`
+and proposes the build tasks and their order; the runtime adds the review
+itself, a critic checks the proposal against `rubrics/plan.md`, and the gate
+at `plan` passes it, a person at L1, before any task is built. The example policy does not grant
+this. To grant it, add a `planning` key naming the most build tasks a
+drafted graph may hold and the roles a planner may seat them under, and a
+scope at `plan` for the roles `planner` and `plan-critic`, with no egress:
+
+```yaml
+planning:
+  maxBuildTasks: 4
+  roles: [builder]
+```
+
+Copy `planner.md` and `plan-critic.md` from
+[packages/compiler/roles/](packages/compiler/roles) into your roles
+directory. The cost `create` prints for such a run is the bound's worst
+case: that many build tasks under the costliest role listed, plus the
+planner, its critic, and the review. A drafted graph past the bound, or
+naming another role, is sent back and never run.
+
 Every run is scanned by two security checks the manifest must pin, each with
 the scanner image the runtime declares for it:
 

===== examples/rubrics/plan.md =====
# Rubric: a drafted task graph

A person owns this file and edits it; the runtime reads it at admission and
gives it to the plan critic. Change it to hold plans to your own standard.

A plan passes only if every point below holds. Each point that does not is a
finding.

1. The tasks together deliver everything the locked spec asks for. A part of
   the spec no task's objective covers is a finding.
2. No task delivers something the spec did not ask for. Extra features,
   refactors, and clean-ups are findings.
3. Each objective says which part of the spec is that task's and where it
   ends, plainly enough that a builder who reads only the spec, its own
   objective, and the objectives of the tasks it depends on knows what to
   build.
4. Two tasks with no dependency between them never need to change the same
   file. They are built at the same time, and one of them would be sent back.
5. Each task depends on every task whose work it needs in place first, and on
   no task it does not need. A dependency that is not needed makes the run
   slower for nothing.
6. The plan uses no more tasks than the spec needs. One task is the right
   plan for a small change.
7. The plan says nothing about how the work is reviewed. The runtime decides
   that.

===== packages/api/rubrics/plan.md =====
# Rubric: a drafted task graph

A person owns this file and edits it; the runtime reads it at admission and
gives it to the plan critic. Change it to hold plans to your own standard.

A plan passes only if every point below holds. Each point that does not is a
finding.

1. The tasks together deliver everything the locked spec asks for. A part of
   the spec no task's objective covers is a finding.
2. No task delivers something the spec did not ask for. Extra features,
   refactors, and clean-ups are findings.
3. Each objective says which part of the spec is that task's and where it
   ends, plainly enough that a builder who reads only the spec, its own
   objective, and the objectives of the tasks it depends on knows what to
   build.
4. Two tasks with no dependency between them never need to change the same
   file. They are built at the same time, and one of them would be sent back.
5. Each task depends on every task whose work it needs in place first, and on
   no task it does not need. A dependency that is not needed makes the run
   slower for nothing.
6. The plan uses no more tasks than the spec needs. One task is the right
   plan for a small change.
7. The plan says nothing about how the work is reviewed. The runtime decides
   that.

 /**
  * Each condition of the drafted floor that does not hold for this draft, by
  * name (D-A-DS-04). A condition the runtime cannot evaluate has failed (I5):
  * a questions part that does not parse has open questions as far as the
  * floor knows, and a critic that was not seated returned no clean result.

 async function conditionsFailed(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, record: DraftRecord): Promise<DraftCondition[]> {
   const failed: DraftCondition[] = [];
   if (record.problems.length > 0) failed.push('shape');
   if (station === 'spec') {
     const questions = fileOf(record, specDraftPaths(ctx.run.id).questions);
     const parsed = questions === undefined ? undefined : parseQuestions(jsonOf(questions), 'questions');
     if (parsed === undefined || !parsed.ok || parsed.part.openQuestions.length > 0) failed.push('no-open-question');
   }
   let clean = false;
   if (current.critic !== null && current.critic !== 'not-seated') {
     const critic = await readRecord<CriticRecord>(ctx, current.critic);
     clean = critic.binding && critic.findings.length === 0;
   }
   if (!clean) failed.push('independent-critic-clean');
   if (station === 'test-design') {
     const passed = current.baseRun !== null && (await judgeBase(ctx, current.baseRun, record)).parts.length === 0;
     if (!passed) failed.push('base-run-passed');
     if (gradeAgainst(record.model, builderModels(ctx)) !== 'different-family') failed.push('drafter-independent-of-builder');
   }
   return failed;
 }

 async function retryParts(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, previous: DraftAttempt | null): Promise<RetryPart[]> {
   const back = previous?.sentBack ?? null;

         ...(own.length > 0 ? [{ kind: 'runtime-problems' as const, problems: own }] : []),

 /**
  * Runs the drafter for the station's current attempt and records what it
  * drafted. The spec drafter works over a fresh copy of the base; the test
  * drafter over a tree holding the locked spec and nothing else, so it is
- * black-box by what it can reach, not by its instructions (I3).
+ * black-box by what it can reach, not by its instructions (I3). The planner
+ * works over a fresh copy of the base, granted the locked spec and never the
+ * intent (R5a, I7).
  */
 async function draft(ctx: LineContext, station: DraftStation, current: DraftAttempt, previous: DraftAttempt | null): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const drivers = draftingDrivers(ctx);
   const driver = seatDriver(drivers, station, 'drafter');
   const seat = seatTask(ctx.state, station, 'drafter', ctx.run.baseCommit);
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], driver.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
   if ((await ctx.components.vault.readLocks(ctx.run.id)) !== null) {
     const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
     if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, tampered, { phase: 'before-draft' }, null);
   }
   // At L3 a test drafter of the builder's family is refused, as a same-family reviewer is (R9, R19).
   if (station === 'test-design') {
     const model = driver.resolveModel(tierFor(scopeFor(ctx, seat), station, attemptsOf(ctx.state, seat)));
     const seated = seatTestDrafter(model, builderModels(ctx), levelOf(ctx.run.requestedLevel, ctx.state.narrowed));
     if (!seated.ok) return seated;
   }
 
   let context: string;
   // The target's instructions, where the drafter is granted them; never at `test-design`, whose contract does not allow them (R24).
   const instructions = await repoInstructionsFor(ctx, seat, STATION_CONTRACTS[station], DRAFT_ROLES[station].drafter);
   if (station === 'spec') {
     context = joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS.spec, withInstructions({ intent: ctx.drafting.intent ?? undefined } as Partial<Record<ContextGrant, string>>, instructions)));
   } else {
     const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
     if (spec.tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, station, null, spec.tampered, { phase: 'context' }, null);
-    context = joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS['test-design'], { 'locked-spec': spec.text }));
+    // The planner is offered the locked spec and the target's instructions, and nothing else: its contract holds no `intent` (D-A-GD-06).
+    context = station === 'plan'
+      ? joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS.plan, withInstructions({ 'locked-spec': spec.text }, instructions)))
+      : joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS['test-design'], { 'locked-spec': spec.text }));

 /** A spec draft's files, each read and parsed from the bytes read; anything wrong is a problem, by path and code. */
 async function readSpecDraft(ctx: LineContext, start: string, work: string): Promise<ReadDraft> {
   const paths = specDraftPaths(ctx.run.id);
   const all = specDraftFiles(ctx);
   const files: DraftFile[] = [];
   const problems: DraftProblem[] = [];
   for (const path of all) {
     const file = await fileAt(work, path);
     if (file === undefined) problems.push({ path, code: 'missing' });
     else files.push(file);
   }
   const spec = files.find((f) => f.path === paths.spec);
   if (spec !== undefined) {
     let text = '';
     try {
       text = new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true }).decode(bytesOf(spec));
     } catch {
       problems.push({ path: paths.spec, code: 'not-utf8' });
     }
     if (text.trim() === '' && !problems.some((p) => p.path === paths.spec)) problems.push({ path: paths.spec, code: 'empty' });
   }
   const parse = (path: string, parser: (value: unknown) => { ok: boolean; problems?: ReadonlyArray<{ path: string; code: string; message: string }> }): void => {
     const file = files.find((f) => f.path === path);
     if (file === undefined) return;
     const json = jsonOf(file);
     if (json === undefined) {
       problems.push({ path, code: 'not-json' });
       return;
     }
     const parsed = parser(json);
     if (!parsed.ok) for (const p of parsed.problems ?? []) problems.push({ path: `${path}#${p.path}`, code: p.code, message: p.message });
   };

   parse(paths.runRequirements, (v) => parseRunRequirements(v, 'runRequirements'));
   parse(paths.questions, (v) => parseQuestions(v, 'questions'));

     // The defect part is held to the runtime's bytes, never parsed for meaning: a reworded part is not the person's (A-R16-03).

     if (defect !== undefined && defect.sha256 !== sha256(defectBytes(ctx.drafting.defect))) problems.push({ path: paths.defect, code: 'not-the-intent' });
     parse(paths.reproduction, (v) => parseReproduction(v, 'reproduction'));
   }
   return { files, discarded: await discardedOf(start, work, (p) => all.includes(p)), problems };

 /**
  * The approved draft written into the artifact root and the base, and
  * locked against its hash (R19). The lock record comes first, with the base
  * digest the draft makes, and is committed before anything is written; then
  * the files, then the Vault's lock of each path not already locked. Every
  * step can be taken again by a resume, and the lock is held to the approved
  * hash: bytes changed in the artifact root after the draft was read are a
  * tamper, never what is locked (I3).

     await writeFiles(scratch, record.files.map((f) => ({ path: f.path, bytes: bytesOf(f) })));
     const digest = await treeDigest(scratch);

+ * read, so a failure here is the validator having changed under the record.

+function draftedGraphOf(ctx: LineContext, record: DraftRecord): TaskGraph {

+/**
+ * Refuses to lock a drafted graph whose worst case is above the bound's
+ * (R5a, I5). The bound's is what admission compared the approved figure
+ * with, and a graph the bound's checks passed cannot cost more: no more
+ * build tasks, each under a role no costlier than the costliest allowed, and
+ * the same one review. This is that argument asserted on the numbers, so a
+ * change to either side that breaks it stops the run before anything of the
+ * graph is written, rather than spending past an approval.
+ */
+function graphWithinBound(ctx: LineContext, record: DraftRecord): void {
+  const stations = DRAFT_STATIONS.filter((station) => ctx.drafting.drafted[station]);
+  const own = worstCaseCost(draftedGraphOf(ctx, record), ctx.policy, stations).usd;
+  const approved = worstCaseCost(boundGraph(graphBoundOf(ctx), ctx.policy, graphBinding(ctx)), ctx.policy, stations).usd;
+  if (own > approved) throw new Error(`line: graph draft ${record.sha256} could cost ${String(own)} USD, above the ${String(approved)} the run was approved for; refusing to lock it`);
+}

  * Reads `FACTORY_CODEX_SEATS`, a comma-separated list of the seats the

  * Codex nowhere. A misspelt or repeated seat stops the host before it listens
  * (I5), as the endpoint file's does.
  */
 export function parseCodexSeats(seats: string, key: string): HostCodex | null {
   if (seats === '' && key === '') return null;
   if (seats === '' || key === '') throw new Error('factory-host: FACTORY_CODEX_SEATS and FACTORY_CODEX_KEY must both be set, or neither');
   const known = new Set<string>(ENDPOINT_SEATS);
   const named: EndpointSeat[] = [];
   for (const seat of seats.split(',').map((s) => s.trim())) {
     if (!known.has(seat)) throw new Error(`factory-host: FACTORY_CODEX_SEATS names the seat ${JSON.stringify(seat)}; the seats are ${ENDPOINT_SEATS.join(', ')}`);
     if (named.includes(seat as EndpointSeat)) throw new Error(`factory-host: FACTORY_CODEX_SEATS names the seat '${seat}' twice`);
     named.push(seat as EndpointSeat);
   }
   return { key, seats: named };
 }

+const NO_TASKS: TaskGraph = Object.freeze({ tasks: [], edges: [] });

+/**
+ * The graph the station machine reads its next step from. A drafted graph
+ * does not exist before `plan` locks it, and up to there the machine reads
+ * tasks only to find a park, so it is given none. Past `plan` a run with no

+export function stepGraph(state: Pick<RunState, 'station' | 'runId'>, graph: TaskGraph | null): TaskGraph {

 /** `build`: one attempt at one task. The result is recorded in the Vault before the task is handed to `verify`. */
 async function build(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {
   const { driver, vault, workspaces } = ctx.components;
   const capability = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS.build, driver.capabilities());
   if (capability !== undefined) return capability;
   const tampered = await tamperedPaths(ctx);
   // The task has not run, so the mismatch is not its role's doing.
   if (tampered.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'build', task, tampered, { phase: 'before-build' }, null);
 
   const spec = await readText(ctx, specArtifacts(ctx));
   const tests = await readText(ctx, ctx.artifacts.acceptanceTests);
   const drifted = [...spec.tampered, ...tests.tampered];
   // Read before the iteration is spent: context that changed under the check costs no budget.
   if (drifted.length > 0) return recordTamper(ctx, 'build', task, drifted, { phase: 'context' }, null);
 
   // Read before the attempt is counted: a gated iteration behind this one is one a gate may have failed (R37).
   const gatedBefore = gatedIterations(attemptsOf(ctx.state, task)) > 0;
   const started = await startAttempt(ctx, task);
   if (started !== undefined) return started;
   // A fresh pair for every invocation, a replayed one included: the tree the task is handed is
   // exactly base and what was accepted, never what an earlier attempt left behind.
   const { start, work } = attemptPaths(workspaces, ctx.run.id, task.id, attemptsOf(ctx.state, task).iterations);
   const base = await accepted(ctx);

   const prepared = await installForBuild(ctx, task, start);

   // What the role is not given is left out of both copies, so the model never has it and its diff never shows it (D-A-TR-04).
   await omitWithheld(ctx, task.role, start);

   const brought = ctx.inputFor?.(task) ?? null;

   // The target's instructions, where this task is granted them, from the admission record alone (R24).
   const instructions = await repoInstructionsFor(ctx, task, STATION_CONTRACTS.build, task.role);
   const inputs = [input, instructions.input].filter((i): i is WithheldInput => i !== undefined);

     'acceptance-tests': tests.text,
-    'task-graph': JSON.stringify(ctx.graph.tasks.map(({ id, station, role, dependsOn }) => ({ id, station, role, dependsOn }))),
+    'task-graph': taskGraphGrant(graphOf(ctx), task),
   };
   // The checks a failed gate named, by id and cause, in the suffix and never the prefix or the role; never their output (I7, R37).
   const suffix = gatedBefore ? await gateRetry(ctx, task) : undefined;
   const ran = await runTask(ctx, task, driver, pinnedRole(ctx, task.role), joinParts(grantedContext(STATION_CONTRACTS.build, withInstructions(offered, instructions))), work, { inputs, ...(suffix === undefined ? {} : { suffix }) });
   if (!ran.ok) return spendRetry(ctx, task, ran.error, ran.budgetSpent);
   // I2: a result is recorded only as the contract shapes it. A key it does not name, `status`
   // among them, is a driver speaking outside the contract; it is not recorded, and the run stops.
   const problems = taskResultProblems(ran.result);
   if (problems.length > 0) {
     throw new Error(`line: the driver's result for task ${task.id} does not match the TaskResult contract, so it was not recorded: ${problems.join('; ')}`);

   await commit(ctx, { tasks: { [task.id]: 'verifying' }, results: { [task.id]: ref } });
   return undefined;
 }

===== packages/api/src/planning.ts =====
/**
 * Drafting at `plan` (R5a). A run admitted with `'draft'` for its task graph
 * seats a planner at `plan`, which proposes the build tasks and their order;
 * the proposal goes through the station's gate and is locked before any
 * builder is seated.
 *
 * What the planner writes is a graph in the shape a person supplies one,
 * held to build tasks alone. The runtime adds the one review task that
 * covers them all, so which work is reviewed is never the author's side's to
 * arrange (I3, D-A-GD-03), and the result goes through `parseGraph`, so
 * nothing a draft produces is a shape the line has not run.
 *
 * The bound is policy's `planning` grant as admission recorded it: the most
 * build tasks, and the roles they may be seated under (I4). Admission
 * approves the worst case of the bound, and no drafted graph inside it can
 * cost more (I5).
 */
import { DERIVED_REVIEW_TASK_ID, REVIEWER_ROLE, StrictPolicyEngine } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { Policy, RoleId, TaskGraph } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { AdmittedGraph } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { DRAFT_DIR } from './spec-parts.js';
import { parseGraph, type GraphBinding } from './validate.js';

/** What a drafted graph is held to: the policy's `planning` grant as the admission record pins it. */
export type GraphBound = Extract<AdmittedGraph, { source: 'drafted' }>['bound'];

/** Where a planner writes its graph, in its tree and in the artifact root alike. */
export function graphDraftPath(runId: string): string {
  return `${DRAFT_DIR}/${runId}/graph.json`;
}

/** A runtime problem with a drafted graph: where, a code, and the validator's own message. */
export interface GraphDraftProblem {
  readonly path: string;
  readonly code: string;
  readonly message: string;
}

function isRecord(value: unknown): value is Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && !Array.isArray(value);
}

/**
 * A drafted graph, bound to its run with the runtime's review task added, or
 * every problem that sends it back. Beyond what `parseGraph` refuses of any
 * graph: a task that is not a build task, a task named as the derived review
 * is, a role outside the bound, more build tasks than the bound, and a build
 * task with no objective.
 */
export function parseDraftedGraph(value: unknown, bind: GraphBinding, bound: GraphBound): { ok: true; graph: TaskGraph } | { ok: false; problems: GraphDraftProblem[] } {
  const raw = isRecord(value) ? value.tasks : undefined;
  if (!Array.isArray(raw) || raw.length === 0) {
    return { ok: false, problems: [{ path: 'graph.tasks', code: 'missing', message: 'graph.tasks must hold at least one build task' }] };
  }
  const entries: readonly unknown[] = raw;
  const problems: GraphDraftProblem[] = [];
  if (entries.length > bound.maxBuildTasks) {
    problems.push({ path: 'graph.tasks', code: 'over-bound', message: `graph.tasks holds ${String(entries.length)} tasks, and policy allows a drafted graph at most ${String(bound.maxBuildTasks)}` });
  }
  entries.forEach((entry, i) => {
    const t = isRecord(entry) ? entry : {};
    const at = `graph.tasks[${String(i)}]`;
    if (t.station === 'review') {
      problems.push({ path: `${at}.station`, code: 'not-a-build-task', message: `${at} is a review task; a drafted graph holds build tasks only, and the runtime adds the review` });
      return;
    }
    if (t.id === DERIVED_REVIEW_TASK_ID) {
      problems.push({ path: `${at}.id`, code: 'reserved', message: `${at}.id '${DERIVED_REVIEW_TASK_ID}' is the review task the runtime adds; a drafted task may not use it` });
    }
    if (typeof t.role === 'string' && t.role !== '' && !bound.roles.includes(t.role as RoleId)) {
      problems.push({ path: `${at}.role`, code: 'role-not-granted', message: `${at}.role '${t.role}' is not a role policy lets a drafted graph seat; the roles are ${bound.roles.join(', ')}` });
    }
    if (t.objective === undefined || t.objective === null) {
      problems.push({ path: `${at}.objective`, code: 'objective-missing', message: `${at}.objective must say what the task delivers` });
    }
  });
  const ids = entries.flatMap((entry) => (isRecord(entry) && entry.station !== 'review' && typeof entry.id === 'string' && entry.id !== '' ? [entry.id] : []));
  const derivedAt = `graph.tasks[${String(entries.length)}]`;
  const derived = { id: DERIVED_REVIEW_TASK_ID, station: 'review', role: REVIEWER_ROLE, dependsOn: [...new Set(ids)], dependencySet: ['**'] };
  const parsed = parseGraph({ tasks: [...entries, derived] }, bind);
  if (!parsed.ok) {
    // What the parser says of the task the runtime added is the runtime's own, and follows from a problem already named.
    for (const p of parsed.problems) if (!p.path.startsWith(derivedAt) && !problems.some((q) => q.path === p.path)) problems.push(p);
  }
  if (problems.length > 0 || !parsed.ok) {
    return { ok: false, problems: problems.length > 0 ? problems : [{ path: 'graph.tasks', code: 'missing', message: 'graph.tasks holds no build task the runtime can review' }] };
  }
  return { ok: true, graph: parsed.graph };
}

const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();

/**
 * The costliest graph the bound allows, as a graph: `maxBuildTasks` build
 * tasks under the allowed role whose call ceiling is highest, the one review
 * task, and its security seat (D-A-GD-02). Every build task makes the same
 * number of calls, so the role with the highest `budget.maxCostUsd` is the
 * costliest. Its worst case is the figure a person approves for a run that
 * drafts its graph. Admission resolved every allowed role's scope first.
 */
export function boundGraph(bound: GraphBound, policy: Policy, bind: GraphBinding): TaskGraph {
  let costliest: { role: RoleId; usd: number } | null = null;
  for (const role of bound.roles) {
    const resolved = engine.resolveCapabilities(role, 'build', policy);
    if (!resolved.ok) throw new Error(`planning: role ${role} has no scope at build: ${resolved.detail}`);
    if (costliest === null || resolved.scope.budget.maxCostUsd > costliest.usd) costliest = { role, usd: resolved.scope.budget.maxCostUsd };
  }
  if (costliest === null || !Number.isSafeInteger(bound.maxBuildTasks) || bound.maxBuildTasks < 1) {
    throw new Error('planning: the bound names no role or no task; policy validation refuses that');
  }
  const role = costliest.role;
  const tasks = Array.from({ length: bound.maxBuildTasks }, (_, i) => ({
    id: `build-${String(i + 1)}`, station: 'build', role, dependsOn: [], dependencySet: ['**'], objective: 'the worst case of one drafted build task',
  }));
  const parsed = parseDraftedGraph({ tasks }, bind, bound);
  if (!parsed.ok) throw new Error(`planning: the bound's own graph does not parse: ${JSON.stringify(parsed.problems)}`);
  return parsed.graph;
}

/** Every role a run seats at `build` and at `review`: those its graph names, or those its bound allows. */
export interface SeatedRoles {
  readonly build: readonly RoleId[];
  readonly review: readonly RoleId[];
}

export function seatedRolesOf(graph: TaskGraph | null, bound: GraphBound | null): SeatedRoles {
  if (graph === null) {
    if (bound === null) throw new Error('planning: a run holds a graph or the bound of a drafted one');
    return { build: [...bound.roles], review: [REVIEWER_ROLE] };
  }
  const at = (station: 'build' | 'review'): RoleId[] => [...new Set(graph.tasks.filter((t) => t.station === station).map((t) => t.role))];
  return { build: at('build'), review: at('review') };
}

+/**
+ * Each task's objective as the locked graph draft states it, from the bytes
+ * the runtime read and recorded (R5a). Empty where the run locked no drafted
+ * graph. Read as data from the record, never parsed for anything else.
+ */
+async function objectivesOf(vault: Vault, state: RunState): Promise<Map<TaskId, string>> {
+  const out = new Map<TaskId, string>();
+  const plan = state.drafts.plan;
+  const lockRef = plan?.locked ?? null;
+  if (plan === undefined || lockRef === null) return out;
+  const lock = decode(await vault.read(lockRef)) as DraftLockRecord;
+  const attempt = plan.attempts.find((a) => a.attempt === lock.draft.attempt);
+  if (attempt?.draft == null) return out;
+  const record = decode(await vault.read(attempt.draft)) as DraftRecord;
+  const file = record.files.find((f) => f.path === graphDraftPath(state.runId));
+  if (file === undefined) return out;
+  const graph = decode(Buffer.from(file.base64, 'base64')) as { tasks?: unknown } | null;
+  const tasks: readonly unknown[] = Array.isArray(graph?.tasks) ? graph.tasks : [];
+  for (const task of tasks) {
+    const t = (typeof task === 'object' && task !== null ? task : {}) as { id?: unknown; objective?: unknown };
+    if (typeof t.id === 'string' && typeof t.objective === 'string') out.set(t.id as TaskId, t.objective);
+  }
+  return out;
+}

 export async function runReport(vault: Vault, runId: RunId): Promise<RunReport> {
   const { state, standing, source } = await runStanding(vault, runId);
   const admission = decode(await vault.read(state.admission)) as { run?: { requestedLevel?: unknown }; template?: RunTemplateId | null; candidate?: { id: CandidateId } | null } | null;
   const requestedLevel = admission?.run?.requestedLevel;
   if (typeof requestedLevel !== 'number') throw new Error(`report: the admission record of run ${runId} names no level`);
 
   const usage = await readUsage(vault, state);
   const cost = costTotals(usage);
   const m = cost.run.metered;
   const input = m.inputTokens + m.cacheReadTokens + m.cacheWriteTokens;
   const calls = usage.filter((r) => r.reading.kind !== 'pending').map((r) => ({ taskId: r.taskId, station: r.station, attempt: r.attempt, model: r.model }));

+  const objectives = await objectivesOf(vault, state);
   const tasks = Object.entries(state.tasks).map(([id, status]) => {
     const taskId = id as TaskId;

+    return { taskId, station: stationOf.get(taskId) ?? null, status, attempts, objective: objectives.get(taskId) ?? null };

   for (const ref of await vault.readDecisions(runId)) decisions.push(decode(await vault.read(ref)) as EnforcementDecision);
   const byCause: Record<string, number> = {};
   const refusals: Record<string, number> = {};
   const parks: Record<string, number> = {};
   for (const d of decisions) {
     bump(byCause, d.decision.cause);
     if (d.decision.cause === 'station-refused') {
       bump(refusals, d.decision.refusal.reason);
       if (d.decision.refusal.reason === 'parked') bump(parks, d.decision.refusal.cause);
     }
   }
 
   const current = M1_STATIONS.indexOf(state.station);
   const stations = M1_STATIONS.map((station, index): StationReport => {
     const attempts = tasks.filter((t) => t.station === station).reduce<TaskAttempts>(
       (sum, t) => ({
         iterations: sum.iterations + t.attempts.iterations,
         retries: sum.retries + t.attempts.retries,
         starts: sum.starts + t.attempts.starts,
         conflicts: [...sum.conflicts, ...t.attempts.conflicts],
       }),
       { iterations: 0, retries: 0, starts: 0, conflicts: [] },
     );
     const at = decisions.filter((d) => d.station === station);
     const stationRefusals: Record<string, number> = {};
     for (const d of at) if (d.decision.cause === 'station-refused') bump(stationRefusals, d.decision.refusal.reason);
     return {
       station,
       gate: gateAt(index, current, standing),
       attempts,
       approvals: at.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'approval-granted').length,
       refusals: stationRefusals,
     };
   });
 
   const mismatches: Array<{ taskId: TaskId; diffSha256: string; differences: readonly string[] }> = [];
   for (const ref of state.evidenceRefs) {
     const bundle = decode(await vault.read(ref)) as EvidenceBundle;
     if (bundle.claimEvidenceDiff.length > 0) mismatches.push({ taskId: bundle.taskId, diffSha256: bundle.diffSha256, differences: bundle.claimEvidenceDiff });
   }
 
   // The issue a run came from, named from the candidate the admission record names (D-A-BG-07).
   const from = admission?.candidate ?? null;
   const entry = from === null ? null : await vault.readCandidate(from.id);
   const candidate = entry?.candidate == null
     ? null
     : { id: entry.candidate.id, kind: entry.candidate.kind, template: entry.candidate.template, extracted: entry.candidate.extracted };
   return {
     runId,
     requestedLevel,
     template: admission?.template ?? null,
     candidate,
     standing,
     standingSource: source,
     cost,
     cacheHitRate: input === 0 ? null : m.cacheReadTokens / input,
     calls,
     tasks,
     stations,
     mismatches,
     decisions: byCause,
     refusals,
     parks,
     integration: await readIntegration(vault, runId),
   };

 /**
  * Why a run's drafting cannot be admitted (R19): an intent where none is
  * drafted from or none where one is, drafting drivers missing, a drafts
  * directory the workspace already holds, and a test drafter or critic
  * granted egress, which would let it reach what the black box keeps from it.
  */
 function draftingProblems(req: RunRequest, drafted: Readonly<Record<DraftStation, boolean>>, policy: Policy, template: RunTemplateId | null): RequestProblem[] {
   const problems: RequestProblem[] = [];
   const intent: unknown = req.intent ?? null;
   if (template === 'bug-fix') problems.push(...bugFixProblems(req));
   else if (isRecord(intent) && Object.hasOwn(intent, 'template')) {
     problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-applicable', message: 'a bug-fix intent is for a bug-fix run, and this run names no template' });
   } else if (drafted.spec) {
     const i = intent as Partial<TextIntent> | null;
     if (i === null) problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'missing', message: 'a run that drafts its spec is drafted from a person\'s intent, and none was given' });
     else if (typeof i !== 'object' || typeof i.text !== 'string' || i.text.trim() === '' || i.from !== 'principal' || Object.keys(i).length !== 2) {
       problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-an-intent', message: 'an intent is a person\'s text from the principal\'s channel: { text, from: \'principal\' }' });
     } else if (Buffer.byteLength(i.text, 'utf8') > INTENT_MAX_BYTES) {
       problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-an-intent', message: `an intent is at most ${String(INTENT_MAX_BYTES)} bytes` });
     }
   } else if (intent !== null) {
     problems.push({ path: 'intent', code: 'not-applicable', message: 'an intent is what a spec is drafted from; a run admitted with its spec takes none' });
   }
   const stations = draftedStations(drafted);
   if (stations.length > 0 && (req.components.drafting ?? null) === null) {
     problems.push({ path: 'components.drafting', code: 'unseated', message: 'a run that drafts needs the drivers that seat each drafter and each critic; none is inferred from the build or review driver' });
   }
   if (stations.length > 0 && (req.rubrics ?? null) === null) {
     problems.push({ path: 'rubrics', code: 'unseated', message: 'a run that drafts needs the rubrics its critics check drafts against' });
   }
   if (drafted['test-design']) {
     for (const seat of ['drafter', 'critic'] as const) {
       const role = DRAFT_ROLES['test-design'][seat];
       const scope = Object.hasOwn(policy.roles, role) ? policy.roles[role] : undefined;
       const egress = scope?.network.egress;
       if (egress !== undefined && egress !== 'none' && egress.length > 0) {
         problems.push({ path: `policy.roles.${role}.network.egress`, code: 'not-black-box', message: `the ${role} is black-box in fact: it reaches the model through its relay and nothing else, so its egress is none` });
       }
     }
   }
+  // The planner reads the locked spec and the base, and reaches nothing beyond its model relay: what it could fetch would be input no grant names (R5a, I7).
+  if (drafted.plan) {
+    for (const seat of ['drafter', 'critic'] as const) {
+      const role = DRAFT_ROLES.plan[seat];
+      const scope = Object.hasOwn(policy.roles, role) ? policy.roles[role] : undefined;
+      const egress = scope?.network.egress;

+        problems.push({ path: `policy.roles.${role}.network.egress`, code: 'not-applicable', message: `the ${role} reaches the model through its relay and nothing else, so its egress is none` });

 }

     }

 /**

  * before anything is admitted, locked, or run (I5): `build`, `review`, and
  * each seat a drafting station holds (R19).
  */

     const refusal = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], driverAt(station, components).capabilities());
     if (refusal !== undefined) return { at: station, refusal };
   }
   const drivers = components.drafting ?? null;
   if (drivers === null) return undefined;
   for (const station of drafted) {
     const drafter = capabilityRefusal(STATION_CONTRACTS[station], seatDriver(drivers, station, 'drafter').capabilities());
     if (drafter !== undefined) return { at: station, refusal: drafter };
     const critic = capabilityRefusal({ ...STATION_CONTRACTS[station], requires: CRITIC_CONTRACTS[station].requires }, seatDriver(drivers, station, 'critic').capabilities());
     if (critic !== undefined) return { at: station, refusal: critic };
   }

 /**
  * Records a human's approval of the exit the run is waiting at, and nothing
  * else. The key must be exactly the station the run is exiting and the level
  * it was admitted at, and the effective approval there must be
  * `human-required`: a `blocked` exit cannot be approved, an `auto` one needs no
  * approval, and a grant for any other station or level would be a standing
  * approval of something nobody has seen yet. A grant already recorded and not
  * yet spent is not duplicated; one already spent does not stand in for the
  * visit now waiting, so a rebuild is approved in its own right (A-P4-04).
  *
  * At a drafted exit the approval names the draft's hash and is refused for
  * any other, and a draft carrying problems the runtime would refuse to lock
  * can be rejected and not approved (R19).
  */
 export async function approveStation(req: ApprovalRequest): Promise<ApprovalOutcome> {
   // Every refusal and every grant is recorded before it takes effect (D-P14-07). The key is kept only when it is one:
   // anything else is the caller's text.
   const key = isApprovalKey(req.key) ? req.key : null;
   const refuse = async (reason: 'invalid-request' | 'not-awaiting' | 'draft-mismatch' | 'draft-invalid', message: string, station: StationId | null): Promise<ApprovalOutcome> => {
     // A run id that is not a non-empty string names nowhere to record the refusal.
     if (typeof req.runId === 'string' && req.runId !== '') {
       await recordDecision({ vault: req.vault, runId: req.runId, taskId: null, station }, { cause: 'approval-refused', decidedBy: 'approval', key, reason });
     }
     return { ok: false, reason, message };
   };
   if (typeof req.approvedBy !== 'string' || req.approvedBy.trim() === '') return refuse('invalid-request', 'approvedBy must name who approved', null);
   if (key === null) return refuse('invalid-request', `'${req.key}' is not a station:level approval key`, null);
   const named: unknown = req.draft ?? null;
   if (named !== null && typeof named !== 'string') return refuse('invalid-request', 'draft must be the hash of the draft approved, or null', null);
   const state = await req.vault.readRunState(req.runId);
   const { record, policy } = await readAdmission(req.vault, state);
   const level = levelOf(record.run.requestedLevel, state.narrowed);
   const { graph, changed, drafting } = await loadLine(req.vault, record, state);
   if (changed.length > 0) return refuse('not-awaiting', `run ${req.runId} has an admitted artifact that changed; it is not awaiting approval`, state.station);
 
   // The policy's own limits: this reads only whether the step is an exit, which the batch size does not change (D-R4-02).
-  const step = nextStep(state, graph, policy.concurrency);
+  const step = nextStep(state, stepGraph(state, graph), policy.concurrency);
   const expected = approvalKey(state.station, level);
   const floor = step.kind === 'exit' ? floorAt(state, drafting) : undefined;
   const draft: DraftRef | null = floor?.draft ?? null;
   // A spent grant for the last exit is a run that passed; there is nothing left to approve.
   const passed = step.kind === 'exit' && !M1_STATIONS.includes(step.to) && state.approvals.some((grant) => grant.key === expected && grant.usedAt !== null);
   const awaiting =
     step.kind === 'exit' && !passed && !isBackward(step.from, step.to) && key === expected &&
     effectiveApproval(STATION_CONTRACTS[state.station], policy, level, [], floor) === 'human-required' &&
     !state.approvals.some((grant) => grantClears(grant, expected, draft));
   if (!awaiting) {
     return refuse('not-awaiting', `run ${req.runId} is not waiting for an approval of ${key}; it is ${state.phase} at ${state.station} at L${String(level)}`, state.station);
   }
   if ((draft?.sha256 ?? null) !== named) {
     return refuse('draft-mismatch', draft === null ? `the exit from ${state.station} approves no draft` : `the draft waiting at ${state.station} is ${draft.sha256}, not ${String(named)}`, state.station);
   }
   if (floor?.failed.includes('shape') === true) {
     return refuse('draft-invalid', `the draft waiting at ${state.station} carries problems the runtime would refuse to lock; it can be rejected, not approved`, state.station);
   }
   // Written before the grant is committed, so a grant never exists without its record; a write that fails throws and grants nothing.
   await recordDecision(
     { vault: req.vault, runId: req.runId, taskId: null, station: state.station },
     { cause: 'approval-granted', decidedBy: 'approval', key, principal: req.approvedBy, draft },
   );
   try {
     const next = await req.vault.commitRunState(
       { ...state, approvals: [...state.approvals, { key, approvedBy: req.approvedBy, approvedAt: new Date().toISOString(), usedAt: null, draft }] },
       state.version,
     );
     return { ok: true, state: next };
   } catch (error) {
     // The grant's record stands beside this refusal: it was decided, and did not take effect.
     return refuse('not-awaiting', `run ${req.runId} moved while the approval was recorded: ${describe(error)}`, state.station);
   }

       state.version,
     );

   } catch (error) {
     return { ok: false, reason: 'not-awaiting', message: `run ${req.runId} moved while the rejection was recorded: ${describe(error)}` };
   }

 /**
  * What a person deciding a draft is shown (R19): the draft's hash and
  * attempt, each condition the runtime found failed, the critic's findings and
  * grade (advisory ones included), and the runtime's own problems with it.
  */
@@ -2087,54 +2157,54 @@ type BareStanding = Exclude<RunStanding, { standing: 'stopped' }> | { readonly s
 async function standingOf(vault: Vault, runId: RunId): Promise<{ state: RunState; standing: BareStanding; source: StandingSource }> {
   const state = await vault.readRunState(runId);
   const { record, policy } = await readAdmission(vault, state);
   // What was committed is read before what the workspace holds now. A cancellation, a halt, and the grant
   // spent in the commit that ended a passed run, are records an artifact edited afterwards does not
   // undo; re-hashed first, a later edit would report either run as tampered (P9 review, codex-3).
   if (state.cancelled !== null || state.halted !== null) {
     // `nextStep` refuses a cancelled or halted run before any other check, so no graph is needed to read why (A-P9-01, A-P9-02).
     const ended = nextStep(state, { tasks: [], edges: [] }, policy.concurrency);
     if (ended.kind === 'refuse') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: ended.refusal }, source: 'records' };
   }
   // The last exit is crossed only by a run that passed, and when it needs an approval the line
   // spends the grant in the commit that ends the run. A spent grant for it is that record; the
   // gate re-evaluated would ask for a second approval of an exit already crossed (D-P9-08).
   const last = approvalKey(LAST_STATION, levelOf(record.run.requestedLevel, state.narrowed));
   if (state.approvals.some((grant) => grant.key === last && grant.usedAt !== null)) return { state, standing: { standing: 'passed' }, source: 'records' };
   const { graph, changed, drafting } = await loadLine(vault, record, state);
   if (changed.length > 0) {
     const paths = changed.map((c) => c.path).join(', ');
     return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: { ok: false, reason: 'lock-tamper', tampered: changed, message: `an admitted artifact changed: ${paths}` } }, source: 'workspace' };
   }
   // The policy's own limits: the standing reads only a refusal or an exit, which the batch size does not change (D-R4-02).
-  const step = nextStep(state, graph, policy.concurrency);
+  const step = nextStep(state, stepGraph(state, graph), policy.concurrency);
   if (step.kind === 'refuse') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: step.refusal }, source: 'workspace' };
   if (step.kind !== 'exit') return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   const floor = floorAt(state, drafting);
   const held = locksHeldLeaving(step.from) && (await vault.readLocks(runId)) !== null;
   const verdict = held ? await vault.verifyLocks(runId) : { ok: true as const };
   // The same escalation the line applies, from the same bundles, so the standing and a drive agree.
   const escalations = step.from === 'integrate'
     ? await integrateEscalations(vault, state, async (ref) => JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(ref))) as EvidenceBundle)
     : { protectedPathsTouched: [], tamperFindings: [] };
   const next = transition({
     from: step.from,
     to: step.to,
     level: levelOf(record.run.requestedLevel, state.narrowed),
     policy,
     tampered: verdict.ok ? [] : verdict.tampered.map((t) => ({ ...t })),
     grants: state.approvals,
     protectedPathsTouched: escalations.protectedPathsTouched,
     tamperFindings: escalations.tamperFindings,
     ...(floor === undefined ? {} : { draft: floor }),
   });
   if (!next.ok) {
     if (next.reason !== 'approval-required') return { state, standing: { standing: 'stopped', refusal: next }, source: 'workspace' };
     const draft = floor === undefined || !isDraftStation(step.from) ? null : await draftStanding(vault, state, step.from, floor);
     return { state, standing: { standing: 'awaiting-approval', key: next.key, escalations: next.escalations, draft }, source: 'workspace' };
   }
   if (M1_STATIONS.includes(next.next)) return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   // Approved, and a pull request opened that is not yet merged: a drive merges it before the run is done (D-I1b-01).
   const integration = await readIntegration(vault, runId);
   if (integration.some((r) => r.kind === 'opened') && !integration.some((r) => r.kind === 'merged')) return { state, standing: { standing: 'open' }, source: 'workspace' };
   return { state, standing: { standing: 'passed' }, source: 'workspace' };
 }

 export interface GraphBinding {
   readonly runId: RunId;

 /**
  * The task graph, bound to its run, or the problems that keep it from being
  * one. At M1 a graph schedules `build` and `review` tasks only. A build task
  * depends only on build tasks; a review task depends on at least one build
  * task and on nothing else; every build task is covered by a review task, so
  * no work reaches `integrate` unreviewed; and the dependencies are acyclic, so
- * the graph can finish.
+ * the graph can finish. A build task may state its objective, in bounded
+ * text; a review task states none, since no review seat is given one
+ * (A-R5a-04).
  */
 export function parseGraph(value: unknown, bind: GraphBinding): { ok: true; graph: TaskGraph } | { ok: false; problems: RequestProblem[] } {
   const { problems, report } = collector();
   const raw = typeof value === 'object' && value !== null && 'tasks' in value ? value.tasks : undefined;
   if (!Array.isArray(raw) || raw.length === 0) {
     report('graph.tasks', 'missing', 'graph.tasks must hold at least one task');
     return { ok: false, problems };
   }
   const entries: readonly unknown[] = raw;
   const ids = new Set<string>();
   const stationOf = new Map<string, string>();
   entries.forEach((entry, i) => {
     const t = (typeof entry === 'object' && entry !== null ? entry : {}) as Record<string, unknown>;
     const at = `graph.tasks[${String(i)}]`;
     if (!isNonEmptyString(t.id)) report(`${at}.id`, 'empty', `${at}.id must be a non-empty string`);
     else if (SEAT_TASK_IDS.includes(t.id as TaskId)) report(`${at}.id`, 'reserved', `${at}.id '${t.id}' is a drafting seat's id in run state; a graph task may not use it (R19)`);
     else if (t.id.endsWith(SECURITY_SEAT_SUFFIX)) report(`${at}.id`, 'reserved', `${at}.id '${t.id}' ends '${SECURITY_SEAT_SUFFIX}', which names the security seat beside a review task; a graph task may not use it (R9)`);
     else if (ids.has(t.id)) report(`${at}.id`, 'duplicate', `${at}.id '${t.id}' is listed twice`);
     else {
       ids.add(t.id);
       if (typeof t.station === 'string') stationOf.set(t.id, t.station);
     }
     if (t.station !== 'build' && t.station !== 'review') {
       report(`${at}.station`, 'bad-station', `${at}.station must be 'build' or 'review'; M1 schedules tasks at no other station`);
     }
     if (!isNonEmptyString(t.role)) report(`${at}.role`, 'empty', `${at}.role must be a non-empty string`);
     // An author running as a reviewer would be the identity that reviews its own change (R9, D-R9-04).
     else if (t.station === 'build' && REVIEWING_ROLES.includes(t.role as RoleId)) {
       report(`${at}.role`, 'reviewing-role', `${at}.role '${t.role}' is a role a review seat runs under; a build task may not author under it (R9)`);
     }
     if (!Array.isArray(t.dependsOn) || !t.dependsOn.every(isNonEmptyString)) {
       report(`${at}.dependsOn`, 'empty', `${at}.dependsOn must be a list of task ids`);
     }
     if (!Array.isArray(t.dependencySet) || t.dependencySet.length === 0 || !t.dependencySet.every(isNonEmptyString)) {
       report(`${at}.dependencySet`, 'missing', `${at}.dependencySet must name at least one glob`);
     }
+    if (t.objective !== undefined && t.objective !== null) {
+      if (t.station === 'review') report(`${at}.objective`, 'not-applicable', `${at}.objective: a review task states no objective; a review seat is never given one`);
+      else if (typeof t.objective !== 'string' || t.objective.trim() === '') report(`${at}.objective`, 'objective-empty', `${at}.objective must be text saying what the task delivers`);
+      else if (Buffer.byteLength(t.objective, 'utf8') > OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES) {
+        report(`${at}.objective`, 'objective-too-long', `${at}.objective is ${String(Buffer.byteLength(t.objective, 'utf8'))} bytes, over the ${String(OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES)} an objective may hold`);
+      }
+    }
   });
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
 
-  const tasks = entries as ReadonlyArray<{ id: string; station: 'build' | 'review'; role: string; dependsOn: string[]; dependencySet: string[] }>;
+  const tasks = entries as ReadonlyArray<{ id: string; station: 'build' | 'review'; role: string; dependsOn: string[]; dependencySet: string[]; objective?: string | null }>;
   const reviewed = new Set<string>();
   tasks.forEach((t, i) => {
     const at = `graph.tasks[${String(i)}].dependsOn`;
     t.dependsOn.forEach((dep, j) => {
       const depStation = stationOf.get(dep);
       if (depStation === undefined) report(`${at}[${String(j)}]`, 'bad-dependency', `${at}[${String(j)}]: '${dep}' is not a task in the graph`);
       else if (depStation !== 'build') {
         report(`${at}[${String(j)}]`, 'bad-dependency', `${at}[${String(j)}]: '${dep}' is a ${depStation} task; tasks depend only on build tasks`);
       } else if (t.station === 'review') reviewed.add(dep);
     });
     if (t.station === 'review' && t.dependsOn.length === 0) {
       report(at, 'bad-dependency', `${at}: a review task must depend on the build task it reviews`);
     }
   });
   tasks.forEach((t, i) => {
     if (t.station === 'build' && !reviewed.has(t.id)) {
       report(`graph.tasks[${String(i)}]`, 'unreviewed', `graph.tasks[${String(i)}]: build task '${t.id}' is covered by no review task`);
     }
   });
   if (!tasks.some((t) => t.station === 'build')) report('graph.tasks', 'missing', 'graph.tasks must hold at least one build task');
   const cycle = findCycle(tasks);
   if (cycle !== undefined) report('graph.tasks', 'cycle', `graph.tasks: the dependencies cycle through ${cycle.join(' -> ')}`);
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, problems };
 
   const bound: Task[] = tasks.map((t) => ({
     id: t.id as TaskId,
     runId: bind.runId,
     station: t.station,
     role: t.role as RoleId,
     dependsOn: t.dependsOn.map((dep) => dep as TaskId),
     baseCommit: bind.baseCommit,
     dependencySet: [...t.dependencySet],
     worktreePath: bind.workspace,
     attempt: 1,
+    objective: t.objective ?? null,
   }));
   // Beside every review task, the security seat: the same task's grants, dependencies, and role, so
   // no graph can leave it out and every counter the line keeps counts it as a task (R9, D-R9-03).
   const seated: Task[] = bound.flatMap((task) => (task.station === 'review' ? [task, { ...task, id: securitySeatId(task.id), dependsOn: [...task.dependsOn], dependencySet: [...task.dependencySet] }] : [task]));
   const edges = seated.flatMap((task) => task.dependsOn.map((dep) => ({ from: dep, to: task.id })));
   return { ok: true, graph: { tasks: seated, edges } };
 }

     const both = worstCaseCost(graph, policy, ['spec', 'test-design']);
     // Three attempts, each with two retries: nine calls a seat.
     expect(spec.tasks.filter((t) => t.station === 'spec').map((t) => [t.task, t.calls, t.usd])).toEqual([['spec.drafter', 9, 4.5], ['spec.critic', 9, 2.25]]);
     expect(spec.usd).toBeCloseTo(without.usd + 6.75);
     expect(both.usd).toBeCloseTo(without.usd + 13.5);
   });
 });

 function declaringAll(graph: BuiltGraph): BuiltGraph {
   const drafting = graph.drafting ?? null;
   return {
     ...graph,
     driver: declaring(graph.driver),
     reviewer: declaring(graph.reviewer),
     ...(drafting === null ? {} : {
       drafting: {
         specDrafter: declaring(drafting.specDrafter), specCritic: declaring(drafting.specCritic),
         testDrafter: declaring(drafting.testDrafter), testCritic: declaring(drafting.testCritic),
+        planner: declaring(drafting.planner), planCritic: declaring(drafting.planCritic),
       },
     }),
   };
 }

   });

===== packages/api/test/planning.test.ts =====
/**
 * R5a's pure pieces: what a drafted graph is held to, the graph the bound's
 * worst case is read from, what an objective may be, and what a builder is
 * given of the graph. The line itself is driven end to end by the
 * conformance registry's R5a assertions.
 */
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { StrictPolicyEngine, type PolicyDocument, type RoleId, type RunId } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import { OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES, boundGraph, graphDraftPath, parseDraftedGraph, parseGraph, taskGraphGrant, worstCaseCost } from '../src/index.js';

const BIND = { runId: 'r' as RunId, baseCommit: 'c', workspace: '/w' };
const BOUND = { maxBuildTasks: 2, roles: ['builder' as RoleId, 'cheap' as RoleId] };

function task(id: string, extra: Record<string, unknown> = {}): Record<string, unknown> {
  return { id, station: 'build', role: 'builder', dependsOn: [], dependencySet: ['**'], objective: `deliver ${id}`, ...extra };
}

function codes(value: unknown): string[] {
  const parsed = parseDraftedGraph(value, BIND, BOUND);
  if (parsed.ok) throw new Error('expected the draft to be sent back');
  return parsed.problems.map((p) => `${p.path}: ${p.code}`);
}

describe('a drafted graph (R5a)', () => {
  it('is its build tasks, one review the runtime adds over all of them, and that review\'s security seat', () => {
    const parsed = parseDraftedGraph({ tasks: [task('a'), task('b', { dependsOn: ['a'] })] }, BIND, BOUND);
    if (!parsed.ok) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(parsed.problems));
    expect(parsed.graph.tasks.map((t) => [t.id, t.station, t.role, t.dependsOn, t.objective])).toEqual([
      ['a', 'build', 'builder', [], 'deliver a'],
      ['b', 'build', 'builder', ['a'], 'deliver b'],
      ['review', 'review', 'reviewer', ['a', 'b'], null],
      ['review.security', 'review', 'reviewer', ['a', 'b'], null],
    ]);
  });

  it('is written at one path under the run\'s drafts directory', () => {
    expect(graphDraftPath('run-1')).toBe('.drafts/run-1/graph.json');
  });

  it('goes back when it is not a list of tasks, or holds none', () => {
    for (const value of [null, 'x', {}, { tasks: 'a' }, { tasks: [] }]) expect(codes(value)).toEqual(['graph.tasks: missing']);
  });

  it('goes back naming a review task, whatever it reviews', () => {
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a'), { id: 'r', station: 'review', role: 'reviewer', dependsOn: ['a'], dependencySet: ['**'] }] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[1].station: not-a-build-task']);
  });

  it('goes back using the id of the review the runtime adds', () => {
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('review')] })).toContain('graph.tasks[0].id: reserved');
  });

  it('goes back naming a role outside the bound, a reviewing role among them', () => {
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { role: 'other' })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].role: role-not-granted']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { role: 'reviewer' })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].role: role-not-granted']);
  });

  it('goes back holding more build tasks than the bound', () => {
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a'), task('b'), task('c')] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks: over-bound']);
  });

  it('goes back when a task states no objective, a blank one, or one over the cap', () => {
    const { objective: _, ...bare } = task('a');
    expect(codes({ tasks: [bare] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].objective: objective-missing']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: null })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].objective: objective-missing']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: '  ' })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].objective: objective-empty']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: 7 })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].objective: objective-empty']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: 'x'.repeat(OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES + 1) })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].objective: objective-too-long']);
    expect(parseDraftedGraph({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: 'x'.repeat(OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES) })] }, BIND, BOUND).ok).toBe(true);
  });

  it('goes back for everything a supplied graph is refused for, and says nothing of the task the runtime added', () => {
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { dependsOn: ['b'] }), task('b', { dependsOn: ['a'] })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks: cycle']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a'), task('a')] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[1].id: duplicate']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { dependsOn: ['nowhere'] })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].dependsOn[0]: bad-dependency']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { station: 'verify' })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].station: bad-station']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('a', { dependencySet: [] })] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].dependencySet: missing']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: [task('plan.drafter')] })).toEqual(['graph.tasks[0].id: reserved']);
    expect(codes({ tasks: ['not a task'] }).every((line) => line.startsWith('graph.tasks[0]'))).toBe(true);
  });
});

describe('an objective on a supplied graph (A-R5a-04)', () => {
  const review = { id: 'v', station: 'review', role: 'reviewer', dependsOn: ['a'], dependencySet: ['**'] };

  it('may be left out, and is then null', () => {
    const { objective: _, ...bare } = task('a');
    const parsed = parseGraph({ tasks: [bare, review] }, BIND);
    if (!parsed.ok) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(parsed.problems));
    expect(parsed.graph.tasks.map((t) => t.objective)).toEqual([null, null, null]);
  });

  it('is held to the same cap, and a review task states none', () => {
    const long = parseGraph({ tasks: [task('a', { objective: 'x'.repeat(OBJECTIVE_MAX_BYTES + 1) }), review] }, BIND);
    expect(long.ok ? [] : long.problems.map((p) => p.code)).toEqual(['objective-too-long']);
    const onReview = parseGraph({ tasks: [task('a'), { ...review, objective: 'review it well' }] }, BIND);
    expect(onReview.ok ? [] : onReview.problems.map((p) => p.code)).toEqual(['not-applicable']);
  });
});

describe('what a builder is given of the graph (A-R5a-04)', () => {
  it('holds every task\'s id, station, role and dependencies, and the objectives of its own task and its dependencies alone', () => {
    const parsed = parseDraftedGraph({ tasks: [task('a'), task('b')] }, BIND, BOUND);
    if (!parsed.ok) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(parsed.problems));
    const c = { id: 'c', dependsOn: ['a'] } as never;
    const given = JSON.parse(taskGraphGrant(parsed.graph, c)) as Array<Record<string, unknown>>;
    expect(given.map((t) => [t.id, t.objective])).toEqual([['a', 'deliver a'], ['b', undefined], ['review', undefined], ['review.security', undefined]]);
    expect(given.every((t) => Object.keys(t).every((key) => ['id', 'station', 'role', 'dependsOn', 'objective'].includes(key)))).toBe(true);
    const own = JSON.parse(taskGraphGrant(parsed.graph, { id: 'b', dependsOn: [] } as never)) as Array<Record<string, unknown>>;
    expect(own.map((t) => t.objective)).toEqual([undefined, 'deliver b', undefined, undefined]);
  });
});

describe('the bound\'s worst case (D-A-GD-02)', () => {
  const scope = (stations: string[], maxCostUsd: number) => ({
    stations, writableGlobs: ['**'], tools: [], network: { egress: 'none' }, tier: 'standard', tierByStation: {}, escalation: 'none', autonomyCeiling: 2, triggerKinds: ['human'],
    budget: { maxTokens: 1, maxCostUsd, maxWallClockMs: 1 }, sensitiveGrants: [], narrowed: null, repoInstructions: false,
  });
  const policy = new StrictPolicyEngine().resolvePolicy({
    globalCap: 2, stationCaps: {}, approvals: {}, protectedPaths: [], sensitivePaths: {},
    roles: { builder: scope(['build'], 1), cheap: scope(['build'], 0.25), reviewer: scope(['review'], 2), planner: scope(['plan'], 3), 'plan-critic': scope(['plan'], 3) },
    triggers: {
      enabled: ['human'], entryStation: { human: 'intake' }, taskTemplate: {}, maxAutonomy: { human: 2 }, minAuthorTrust: { human: 'owner' }, narrowBelow: {},
      maxTriggerDepth: 2, budgetPerWindow: { runs: 1, windowMs: 1 },
    },
    concurrency: { maxParallelTasks: 1, maxConflictRetries: 0 },
    planning: { maxBuildTasks: 2, roles: ['cheap', 'builder'] },
  } as unknown as PolicyDocument);

  it('is the most build tasks under the costliest allowed role, one review, and its security seat', () => {
    const graph = boundGraph({ maxBuildTasks: 2, roles: ['cheap' as RoleId, 'builder' as RoleId] }, policy, BIND);
    expect(graph.tasks.map((t) => [t.station, t.role])).toEqual([['build', 'builder'], ['build', 'builder'], ['review', 'reviewer'], ['review', 'reviewer']]);
  });

  it('is never below the worst case of a graph the bound admits', () => {
    const bound = { maxBuildTasks: 2, roles: ['cheap' as RoleId, 'builder' as RoleId] };
    const figure = worstCaseCost(boundGraph(bound, policy, BIND), policy, ['plan']).usd;
    for (const tasks of [[task('a')], [task('a', { role: 'cheap' })], [task('a'), task('b', { role: 'cheap' })], [task('a'), task('b')]]) {
      const parsed = parseDraftedGraph({ tasks }, BIND, bound);
      if (!parsed.ok) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(parsed.problems));
      expect(worstCaseCost(parsed.graph, policy, ['plan']).usd).toBeLessThanOrEqual(figure);
    }
    const full = parseDraftedGraph({ tasks: [task('a'), task('b')] }, BIND, bound);
    if (!full.ok) throw new Error('the full graph did not parse');
    expect(worstCaseCost(full.graph, policy, ['plan']).usd).toBe(figure);
  });

  it('counts the planner and the plan critic among its seats', () => {
    const bound = { maxBuildTasks: 1, roles: ['builder' as RoleId] };
    const seats = worstCaseCost(boundGraph(bound, policy, BIND), policy, ['plan']).tasks.filter((t) => t.station === 'plan');
    expect(seats.map((t) => [t.task, t.role])).toEqual([['plan.drafter', 'planner'], ['plan.critic', 'plan-critic']]);
  });
});

 /** One drafted item through the drafting station, with `critic` seated. */
 export async function throughTheCritic(bench: Bench, corpus: Corpus, item: Item, critic: CriticSeat): Promise<CriticOutcome> {
   const station = stationOf(item);
   const source = await installed(bench, corpus, item.repo);
   const name = randomUUID();
   const root = join(bench.base, 'runs', name);
   const repository = join(root, 'repository');
   await cp(source.dir, repository, { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'store'), { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(join(root, 'trees'), { recursive: true });
   const vault = new LocalVault({ store: join(root, 'store'), artifacts: repository });
   const runId = name as RunId;
   const drafter = new ScriptedSeat(bench.provider, (station === 'spec' ? 'spec-drafter' : 'test-drafter') as RoleId, item.change, item.claim, name);
   const idle = new BenchStub();
   const components = buildGraph({
     vault,
     sandbox: bench.provider,
     driver: idle,
     reviewer: idle,
     drafting: station === 'spec'
-      ? { specDrafter: drafter, specCritic: critic.driver, testDrafter: idle, testCritic: idle }
-      : { specDrafter: idle, specCritic: idle, testDrafter: drafter, testCritic: critic.driver },
+      ? { specDrafter: drafter, specCritic: critic.driver, testDrafter: idle, testCritic: idle, planner: idle, planCritic: idle }
+      : { specDrafter: idle, specCritic: idle, testDrafter: drafter, testCritic: critic.driver, planner: idle, planCritic: idle },
     workspaces: localWorkspaceStore({ root: join(root, 'trees'), user: bench.user }),
     integrator: null,
     profile: { buildImage: critic.image, checkImage: BASE_IMAGE, limits: DEFAULT_LIMITS },
   });
   const policy = draftingPolicy(corpus, item, critic);
   const artifacts = draftedArtifacts(station);
   // Bound as admission binds it, so the security seat it derives is in the figure (R9).
   const graph = boundGraph(JSON.parse(await readFile(join(repository, artifacts.taskGraph), 'utf8')), { runId, baseCommit: source.commit, workspace: repository });
   if (item.asked === null) throw new Error(`benchmark: drafted item '${item.id}' carries no request`);
   const outcome = await startRun({
     runId,
     baseCommit: source.commit,
     requestedLevel: 1,
     workspace: repository,
     artifacts,
     policy,
     roles: DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY,
     components,
     readiness: BENCH_READINESS,
     intent: station === 'spec' ? { text: item.asked, from: 'principal' } : null,
     rubrics: DEFAULT_RUBRICS_DIRECTORY,
     approvedCostUsd: worstCaseCost(graph, policy, [station]).usd,
   });
   if (!outcome.ok && outcome.reason !== 'refused') {
     throw new Error(`benchmark: the drafted run was refused before the line started (${outcome.reason}); the corpus or the bench is broken: ${JSON.stringify(outcome)}`);
   }
   const state = await vault.readRunState(runId);
   const usage = await readUsage(vault, state);
   // A call whose cost was lost or never read cannot be charged against the cap, so the run stops (I5); an unmetered one is the scripted seat's and cost nothing.
   const totals = costTotals(usage).run;
   const unsettled = unsettledCalls(usage).length;
   if (unsettled > 0 || totals.lost > 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: ${String(unsettled + totals.lost)} call(s) of the drafted run have no known cost, so the spend cannot be held to the cap`);
   const costUsd = totals.metered.costUsd;
   const first = state.drafts[station]?.attempts[0];
   if (first?.draft === null || first === undefined) throw new Error(`benchmark: the drafted run for an item recorded no first draft at ${station}`);
   const record = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(first.draft))) as DraftRecord;
   const problems = record.problems.map((p) => p.code);
   let answer: { readable: boolean; findings: number } | null = null;
   if (first.critic === null || first.critic === 'not-seated') {
     // The runtime sent the draft back before a critic read it; the catch is the runtime's.
     if (problems.length === 0) throw new Error(`benchmark: the critic was ${String(first.critic)} on a draft the runtime did not send back; the arm measured nothing. The run stopped with: ${outcome.ok ? 'no refusal' : JSON.stringify({ at: outcome.at, transition: outcome.transition })}`);
   } else {
     const verdict = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await vault.read(first.critic))) as CriticRecord;
     if (!verdict.binding) throw new Error('benchmark: the critic seated was not binding: its family is the drafter\'s, so its findings send nothing back');
     answer = { readable: verdict.readable, findings: verdict.findings.length };
   }
   return { ...criticVerdict(problems, answer, first.sentBack?.by ?? null), costUsd };
 }

 async function create(io: CliIo, t: Target, args: string[]): Promise<ExitCode> {
   const { values, positionals } = parseOwn('create', args);
   if (positionals.length > 0) throw new UsageError(`create takes no positional arguments: ${positionals.join(' ')}`);
   const spec = strs(values, 'spec');
   const tests = strs(values, 'tests');
   // A drafted artifact is drafted on the line instead of named here (R19); naming both is a contradiction.
   if (bool(values, 'draft-spec') && spec !== undefined) throw new UsageError('--draft-spec and --spec name the spec twice');
   if (bool(values, 'draft-tests') && tests !== undefined) throw new UsageError('--draft-tests and --tests name the acceptance tests twice');
+  if (bool(values, 'draft-plan') && str(values, 'graph') !== undefined) throw new UsageError('--draft-plan and --graph name the task graph twice');
   const security = str(values, 'security-part');
   const runRequirements = str(values, 'run-requirements');
   const questions = str(values, 'questions');
   const parts = {
     ...(security === undefined ? {} : { security }),
     ...(runRequirements === undefined ? {} : { runRequirements }),
     ...(questions === undefined ? {} : { questions }),
   };
   const acceptanceManifest = str(values, 'acceptance-manifest');
   const intent = str(values, 'intent');
   const body: CreateRunBody = {
     workspace: required(str(values, 'workspace'), '--workspace'),
     baseCommit: required(str(values, 'base-commit'), '--base-commit'),
     requestedLevel: level(str(values, 'level')),
     artifacts: {
       spec: bool(values, 'draft-spec') ? 'draft' : (spec ?? []),
       acceptanceTests: bool(values, 'draft-tests') ? 'draft' : (tests ?? []),
       verificationManifest: required(str(values, 'manifest'), '--manifest'),
-      taskGraph: required(str(values, 'graph'), '--graph'),
+      // A drafted graph is drafted at `plan`, within the policy's planning grant (R5a).
+      taskGraph: bool(values, 'draft-plan') ? 'draft' : required(str(values, 'graph'), '--graph'),
       ...(acceptanceManifest === undefined ? {} : { acceptanceManifest }),
       // Optional on the wire: a spec created without its security part is refused at the spec exit, naming the part (R17).
       ...(Object.keys(parts).length === 0 ? {} : { specParts: parts }),
     },
     approvedCostUsd: cost(str(values, 'approve-cost')),
     ...(intent === undefined ? {} : { intent }),
   };
   const res = await call(io, t, 'POST', 'runs', body);
   const unapproved = costRefusal(res.body);
   if (unapproved !== undefined) {
     const { usd, calls } = unapproved.worstCase;
     io.err(`the worst-case cost of this run is $${String(usd)}, over at most ${String(calls)} driver calls`);
     const likely = likelyLine(unapproved.likely);
     if (likely !== undefined) io.err(likely);
     io.err(body.approvedCostUsd === null
       ? `nothing was started; to approve it, run the same command with --approve-cost ${String(usd)}`
       : `nothing was started; --approve-cost ${String(body.approvedCostUsd)} is not that figure`);
     return 1;
   }
   return report(io, res);
 }

===== packages/compiler/roles/plan-critic.md =====
# Plan critic

You read a drafted task graph and say what is wrong with it. You did not write
it, and you change nothing: your working directory is read-only.

## What you work from

Your context holds the locked spec the graph was drafted from, the draft
itself (one file, under its path), and the rubric a person wrote for this
check. The repository is in your working directory, with the draft's file in
it.

## What to check

Check the draft against the rubric. At the least:

- Do the tasks together deliver everything the spec asks for, and nothing
  beyond it?
- Does each objective say which part of the spec is that task's, clearly
  enough that its builder knows where its work ends?
- Could two tasks with no dependency between them need to change the same
  file?
- Does each task depend on the tasks whose work it needs, and on no others?
- Is the work split more finely, or more coarsely, than the spec needs?

## What to say

Your final message is your result, and nothing else: one JSON object,

```
{"findings": [{"path": "...", "note": "..."}]}
```

Each finding is one problem. `path` is the draft file it is in, exactly as
the draft names it, and `note` says the problem so the planner can fix it.
Give no severity and no praise. If you found nothing wrong, return
`{"findings": []}`. Anything other than that object is counted as a finding,
and so is a finding whose `path` is not a file of the draft.

===== packages/compiler/roles/planner.md =====
# Planner

You draft the task graph for one run: the build tasks that deliver the spec,
and the order between them. The message you are given is your seat's id and
the attempt you are on. On a later attempt it also says why the last draft
went back.

## What you work from

Your context holds the locked spec: what the run must deliver, and what it is
judged by. The repository is in your working directory, so you can read how it
is laid out and where the work will land. It is a copy: nothing you change in
it leaves your seat except the one file below.

## What you write

Write exactly one file, at the path under `.drafts/` that your request names:

```
{ "tasks": [
  { "id": "...", "station": "build", "role": "...",
    "dependsOn": ["..."], "dependencySet": ["..."], "objective": "..." }
] }
```

- `id`: a short name, unique in the graph. Not `review`, and nothing ending
  in `.security`: the runtime adds the review itself.
- `station`: always `build`. You plan the building. You do not plan the
  review.
- `role`: the role that builds the task. Your request names the roles you may
  use and how many tasks the graph may hold. Use no other role, and no more
  tasks.
- `dependsOn`: the ids of the tasks whose work this task needs in place
  first. Leave it empty when the task needs none. Tasks that do not depend on
  each other are built at the same time, so two of them must never need to
  change the same file. No cycles.
- `dependencySet`: globs for the files the task reads and writes. At least
  one.
- `objective`: what this task delivers, in a few plain sentences. The builder
  of this task reads it, and so does the builder of every task that depends
  on it. Say which part of the spec is this task's, and where it ends. Keep
  it short: a long objective is refused.

## How to split the work

- Use the fewest tasks that let independent work run side by side. One task
  is a good plan for a small spec.
- Give each task work that can pass the spec's tests for its part once the
  tasks it depends on are in place.
- Put work that touches the same files in one task, or order it with
  `dependsOn`.
- Plan what the spec asks for and nothing beyond it.

## What you must not do

- Do not write review tasks, name reviewers, or say what is reviewed.
- Do not write code, tests, or anything else in the tree. Nothing else you
  write is read.
- Do not reach for anything the session does not offer you.

## How you are judged

The runtime reads the one file, checks its shape and its bounds, and records
the bytes it read. A critic checks the draft against a rubric a person wrote,
and a person or the policy decides whether it is approved. Nothing you say
decides it.

## What to say when you finish

Say what you planned, and say plainly what you were unsure of. Do not describe
work you did not do.
