 /**
  * The only path from an unvalidated value to a `PolicyDocument`. Returns every
  * defect it finds; an `ok: true` result means nothing was wrong, not that
  * something was repaired.
  */
 export function validatePolicyDocument(input: unknown): DocumentValidation {
   const defects: PolicyDefect[] = [];
   const record = shape(input, '', keysOf(DOCUMENT_KEYS), defects);
   if (record === undefined) return { ok: false, defects };
 
   const globalCap = read<AutonomyLevel>(record, 'globalCap', '', 'an autonomy level 0-3', isAutonomyLevel, defects);
   const stationCaps = readPartialMap<StationId, AutonomyLevel>(
     record, 'stationCaps', '', 'a station id', isStationId, 'an autonomy level 0-3', isAutonomyLevel, defects,
   );
   const approvals = readPartialMap<ApprovalKey, ApprovalOutcome>(
     record, 'approvals', '', `a 'station:level' key`, isApprovalKey,
     `one of 'auto', 'human-required', 'blocked'`, isApprovalOutcome, defects,
   );
   const protectedPaths = readArray<string>(record, 'protectedPaths', '', 'a non-empty path or glob', isNonEmptyString, defects);
   // Absent from a document written before R10: no path is sensitive (A-R10-01).
   const sensitivePaths = Object.hasOwn(record, 'sensitivePaths') ? validateSensitivePaths(record.sensitivePaths, defects) : {};
   // Absent from a document written before R34: no install may reach anything (A-R34-01, I4). Each entry is one host, as an egress entry is.
   const installRegistries = Object.hasOwn(record, 'installRegistries')
     ? readArray<string>(record, 'installRegistries', '', 'one host: no wildcard, path, or whitespace', (v): v is string => isNonEmptyString(v) && isHostLiteral(v), defects)
     : [];
   // Absent from a document written before R25: no rule needs a property check (D-R25-05).
   const propertyPerAuthorizationRule = Object.hasOwn(record, 'propertyPerAuthorizationRule')
     ? read<boolean>(record, 'propertyPerAuthorizationRule', '', 'true or false', (x): x is boolean => typeof x === 'boolean', defects)
     : false;
   const roles = Object.hasOwn(record, 'roles')
     ? validateRoles(record.roles, 'roles', defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'roles')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'roles', problem: 'required key is missing; expected an object keyed by role id' });
   }
   const triggers = Object.hasOwn(record, 'triggers')
     ? validateTriggers(record.triggers, 'triggers', defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'triggers')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'triggers', problem: 'required key is missing; expected a trigger policy' });
   }
   const concurrencyRecord = Object.hasOwn(record, 'concurrency')
     ? shape(record.concurrency, 'concurrency', keysOf(CONCURRENCY_KEYS), defects)
     : undefined;
   if (!Object.hasOwn(record, 'concurrency')) {
     defects.push({ path: 'concurrency', problem: 'required key is missing; expected { maxParallelTasks, maxConflictRetries }' });
   }
   const maxParallelTasks = concurrencyRecord === undefined
     ? undefined
     : read(concurrencyRecord, 'maxParallelTasks', 'concurrency', 'a positive integer', isPositiveInteger, defects);
   const maxConflictRetries = concurrencyRecord === undefined
     ? undefined
     : read(concurrencyRecord, 'maxConflictRetries', 'concurrency', 'a non-negative integer', isNonNegativeInteger, defects);
 
   if (
     globalCap === undefined || stationCaps === undefined || approvals === undefined
     || protectedPaths === undefined || sensitivePaths === undefined || installRegistries === undefined || propertyPerAuthorizationRule === undefined || roles === undefined || triggers === undefined
     || maxParallelTasks === undefined || maxConflictRetries === undefined
     || defects.length > 0
   ) {
     // A defect with no undefined field is still a refusal: `shape` reports an
     // unknown key without failing the fields around it.
     return { ok: false, defects };
   }
   // A grant names a class the policy declares; one it does not would mount nothing and read as a grant (I4).
   for (const [roleId, scope] of Object.entries(roles)) {
     scope.sensitiveGrants.forEach((grant, i) => {
       if (!Object.hasOwn(sensitivePaths, grant)) {
         defects.push({ path: `roles.${roleId}.sensitiveGrants[${String(i)}]`, problem: `'${grant}' is not a class sensitivePaths declares` });
       }
     });
   }
-  if (defects.length > 0) return { ok: false, defects };
+  // Absent or null in a document written before R5a, and in a resolved policy that grants none: no graph is drafted (A-R5a-02, I4).
+  const planning = Object.hasOwn(record, 'planning') && record.planning !== null ? validatePlanning(record.planning, roles, defects) : null;
+  if (defects.length > 0 || planning === undefined) return { ok: false, defects };
   return {
     ok: true,
     document: {
       globalCap, stationCaps, approvals, roles, protectedPaths, sensitivePaths, triggers,
       concurrency: { maxParallelTasks, maxConflictRetries },
       installRegistries,
       propertyPerAuthorizationRule,
+      ...(planning === null ? {} : { planning }),
     },
   };
 }
 
+/**
+ * The planning grant (A-R5a-02): at least one build task, and at least one
+ * role, each defined, each granted `build`, none a reviewing role, none
+ * listed twice. A role a planner may seat a builder under that has no scope
+ * at `build` would be a task no policy lets run, and a reviewing role would
+ * seat an author as the identity that reviews it (R9).
+ */
+function validatePlanning(value: unknown, roles: Readonly<Record<RoleId, CapabilityScope>>, defects: PolicyDefect[]): PlanningGrant | undefined {
+  const before = defects.length;
+  const record = shape(value, 'planning', keysOf(PLANNING_KEYS), defects);
+  if (record === undefined) return undefined;
+  const maxBuildTasks = read(record, 'maxBuildTasks', 'planning', 'a positive integer', isPositiveInteger, defects);
+  const named = readArray<string>(record, 'roles', 'planning', 'a role id', isNonEmptyString, defects);
+  if (named?.length === 0) defects.push({ path: 'planning.roles', problem: 'a planner with no role to seat a builder under can draft no graph' });
+  (named ?? []).forEach((role, i) => {
+    const at = `planning.roles[${String(i)}]`;
+    if (named?.indexOf(role) !== i) defects.push({ path: at, problem: `'${role}' is listed twice` });
+    else if (REVIEWING_ROLES.includes(role as RoleId)) defects.push({ path: at, problem: `'${role}' is a role a review seat runs under; a build task may not author under it` });
+    else if (!Object.hasOwn(roles, role)) defects.push({ path: at, problem: `'${role}' is not a role this policy defines` });
+    else if (roles[role as RoleId]?.stations.includes('build') !== true) defects.push({ path: at, problem: `'${role}' is granted no scope at build` });
+  });
+  if (maxBuildTasks === undefined || named === undefined || defects.length > before) return undefined;
+  return { maxBuildTasks, roles: named as RoleId[] };
+}

 /** Whether `glob` can match a path as the runtime names one: relative to the tree's root, `/`-separated, never leaving it. */
 function isTreeRelativeGlob(glob: string): boolean {
   if (glob.startsWith('/') || glob.startsWith('./') || glob.includes('\\') || /^[A-Za-z]:/u.test(glob)) return false;

 /** The refusal of a run whose drafting station's last allowed draft a person rejected (R19, D-A-DS-02). */
@@ -583,42 +584,42 @@ function retryMessage(message: string): string {
 /**
  * A drafter's retry as its request's variable suffix: the typed parts of
  * what sent its last draft back, and nothing else (D-A-DS-02). Never in the
  * stable prefix or a role. At `test-design` the locked-spec-alone rule
  * admits these parts and no other: a person's notes there are refused, since
  * an approver who has read the repository would otherwise be a channel
  * around the black box, and a test drafter learns of the base only that its
  * draft did not hold there, never which check, path, or exit code (A-R19-11).
  */
 export function retrySuffix(station: DraftStation, attempt: number, parts: readonly RetryPart[]): string {
   const lines = [`attempt ${String(attempt)}`];
   for (const part of parts) {
     switch (part.kind) {
       case 'critic-findings':
         lines.push('[critic-findings]', ...part.findings.map((f) => `- ${f}`));
         break;
       case 'runtime-problems':
         lines.push('[runtime-problems]', ...part.problems.map((p) => `- ${p.path}: ${p.code}${p.message === undefined ? '' : ` — ${retryMessage(p.message)}`}`));
         break;
       case 'base-refusal':
         lines.push('[base-refusal]', '- the draft did not hold over the repository; nothing more about the repository is told');
         break;
       case 'rejection-reason':
         lines.push('[rejection-reason]', part.reason);
         break;
       case 'notes':
-        if (station !== 'spec') throw new Error(`station machine: a ${station} retry carries no notes; a rejection there carries a reason from a fixed set`);
+        if (station === 'test-design') throw new Error(`station machine: a ${station} retry carries no notes; a rejection there carries a reason from a fixed set`);
         lines.push('[approver-notes]', part.notes);
         break;
     }
   }
   return lines.join('\n');
 }
 
 /**
  * A build retry after a failed gate, as a part of its request's variable
  * suffix: each failed check's id and the cause the gate derived, typed, and
  * nothing else (R37). Never its exit code, and never a byte the check or its
  * container printed: that text comes from code the builder wrote, so it could
  * carry instructions into the builder's own next prompt (I7). The ids are the
  * locked manifest's, which the builder already reads and cannot write (I3).
  */

+
+describe('the planning grant (A-R5a-02)', () => {
+  /** A document with one build role, one review role, and `planning` as given. */
+  function planned(planning: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
+    const document = authored();
+    document.roles = { builder: scope(), reviewer: { ...scope(), stations: ['review'] }, drafter: { ...scope(), stations: ['spec'] } };
+    document.planning = planning;
+    return document;
+  }
+
+  test('is accepted with a positive bound and roles granted build, and absent or null it grants nothing', () => {
+    expect(accept(planned({ maxBuildTasks: 3, roles: ['builder'] })).planning).toEqual({ maxBuildTasks: 3, roles: ['builder'] });
+    expect(accept(planned(null)).planning).toBeUndefined();
+    expect(accept(authored()).planning).toBeUndefined();
+  });
+
+  test('refuses zero build tasks, and a bound that is not a whole number', () => {
+    for (const maxBuildTasks of [0, -1, 1.5, '3']) {
+      expect(lines(refuse(planned({ maxBuildTasks, roles: ['builder'] }))).join('\n')).toContain('planning.maxBuildTasks: expected a positive integer');
+    }
+  });
+
+  test('refuses an empty role list', () => {
+    expect(lines(refuse(planned({ maxBuildTasks: 1, roles: [] })))).toContainEqual('planning.roles: a planner with no role to seat a builder under can draft no graph');
+  });
+
+  test('refuses a role the policy does not define, one with no scope at build, a reviewing role, and a role listed twice', () => {
+    const reported = lines(refuse(planned({ maxBuildTasks: 1, roles: ['nobody', 'drafter', 'reviewer', 'security-reviewer', 'builder', 'builder'] })));
+    expect(reported).toEqual([
+      "planning.roles[0]: 'nobody' is not a role this policy defines",
+      "planning.roles[1]: 'drafter' is granted no scope at build",
+      "planning.roles[2]: 'reviewer' is a role a review seat runs under; a build task may not author under it",
+      "planning.roles[3]: 'security-reviewer' is a role a review seat runs under; a build task may not author under it",
+      "planning.roles[5]: 'builder' is listed twice",
+    ]);
+  });
+
+  test('refuses an unknown key, a missing one, and a value that is not an object', () => {
+    expect(lines(refuse(planned({ maxBuildTasks: 1, roles: ['builder'], perTask: 2 }))).join('\n')).toContain('planning.perTask');
+    expect(lines(refuse(planned({ roles: ['builder'] }))).join('\n')).toContain('planning.maxBuildTasks');
+    expect(lines(refuse(planned(3))).join('\n')).toContain('planning');
+  });
+});

===== scripts/local-check.ts =====
diff --git a/scripts/local-check.ts b/scripts/local-check.ts
index 961f2fd..aba175d 100644
--- a/scripts/local-check.ts
+++ b/scripts/local-check.ts
@@ -136,24 +136,54 @@ export function classify(outcomes: readonly TestOutcome[], problems: readonly st
 /** The outcomes in a vitest JSON report. Anything not shaped like one throws: an unreadable report is not a pass. */
 export function outcomesOf(report: unknown): TestOutcome[] {
   const files = (report as { testResults?: unknown } | null)?.testResults;
   if (!Array.isArray(files)) throw new Error('local-check: the test report has no testResults list');
   const out: TestOutcome[] = [];
   for (const entry of files as unknown[]) {
     const { name, status, assertionResults } = entry as { name?: unknown; status?: unknown; assertionResults?: unknown };
     if (typeof name !== 'string' || !Array.isArray(assertionResults)) throw new Error('local-check: a test file in the report is malformed');
     let failedTests = 0;
     for (const result of assertionResults as unknown[]) {
       const { title, status: testStatus } = result as { title?: unknown; status?: unknown };
       if (typeof title !== 'string' || typeof testStatus !== 'string') throw new Error(`local-check: a test in ${name} is malformed`);
       if (testStatus === 'failed') failedTests += 1;
       out.push({ file: name, title, status: testStatus });
     }
     // A file that failed with no failed test failed outside one: a broken import, a thrown hook.
     if (status === 'failed' && failedTests === 0) out.push({ file: name, title: '(the file failed outside any test)', status: 'failed' });
   }
   return out;
 }
 
+/**
+ * What a conformance run's exit status and printed output say that its
+ * report's tests do not (#249). A runner that exits non-zero with no failed
+ * test in its report failed outside one. So did a runner that printed an
+ * unhandled error, whatever else failed beside it: a stale paid report is an
+ * excuse for the entries it names and for nothing else. An exit of zero
+ * beside a failed test is a report this cannot trust either.
+ */
+export function runnerProblems(status: number, output: string, outcomes: readonly TestOutcome[]): string[] {
+  // eslint-disable-next-line no-control-regex -- the escape character is what a colour code starts with
+  const lines = output.replace(/\u001b\[[0-9;]*m/gu, '').split(/\r?\n/u);
+  const problems: string[] = [];
+  // The headings vitest prints over an error no test owns, alone on their line between rules.
+  if (lines.some((line) => /^[\s\u23AF]*Unhandled (Errors?|Rejection)[\s\u23AF]*$/u.test(line))) problems.push('runner: vitest reported an error outside any test');
+  const failed = outcomes.some((o) => o.status === 'failed');
+  if (status !== 0 && !failed) problems.push(`runner: vitest exited ${String(status)} with no failed test in its report`);
+  if (status === 0 && failed) problems.push('runner: vitest exited 0 with a failed test in its report');
+  return problems;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Why a whole-suite run proves nothing, or an empty list (#249). The
+ * acceptance run is every test: one that ran none, or left any unexecuted,
+ * has not shown what `full` says it shows.
+ */
+export function unexecuted(outcomes: readonly TestOutcome[]): string[] {
+  if (!outcomes.some((o) => o.status === 'passed')) return ['runner: no test passed, so the suite did not run'];
+  return outcomes.filter((o) => o.status !== 'passed' && o.status !== 'failed').map((o) => `not executed (${o.status}): ${o.file}: ${o.title}`);
+}
+
 /** A registry id as this script accepts one: what the registry uses, and nothing a pattern could misread. */
 export function isRegistryId(id: string): boolean {
   return /^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9]*(\.[A-Za-z0-9-]+)+$/u.test(id);

 async function full(steps: Step[]): Promise<void> {
   if (!(await staticSteps(steps, true))) return;

   for (const pkg of testedPackages()) {

   }

+describe('what the runner says beyond its tests (#249)', () => {
+  const passed = local('I9.x', 'passed');
+
+  test('a clean exit over passed tests is no problem, and neither is a failing exit over a failed test', () => {
+    expect(runnerProblems(0, ' \u2713 a test\n', [passed])).toEqual([]);
+    expect(runnerProblems(1, ' \u00d7 a test\n', [passed, PAID])).toEqual([]);
+  });
+
+  test('a non-zero exit with no failed test is a failure', () => {
+    expect(runnerProblems(1, '', [passed])).toEqual(['runner: vitest exited 1 with no failed test in its report']);
+  });
+
+  test('an unhandled error is a failure even beside a failed paid entry, which excuses only itself', () => {
+    const output = ['\u001b[31m\u23AF\u23AF\u23AF\u23AF Unhandled Errors \u23AF\u23AF\u23AF\u23AF\u001b[39m', '', '\u23AF\u23AF Unhandled Rejection \u23AF\u23AF'].join('\n');
+    expect(runnerProblems(1, output, [passed, PAID])).toEqual(['runner: vitest reported an error outside any test']);
+  });
+
+  test('a test whose title mentions an unhandled error is not one', () => {
+    expect(runnerProblems(0, ' \u2713 [I5.x] an Unhandled Rejection is recorded\n', [passed])).toEqual([]);
+  });
+
+  test('an exit of zero beside a failed test is not trusted', () => {
+    expect(runnerProblems(0, '', [PAID])).toEqual(['runner: vitest exited 0 with a failed test in its report']);
+  });
+});
+
+describe('a whole-suite run that did not run (#249)', () => {
+  test('every test passed or failed: nothing is unexecuted', () => {
+    expect(unexecuted([local('I9.x', 'passed'), PAID])).toEqual([]);
+  });
+
+  test('an empty report, or one with no passed test, did not run', () => {
+    expect(unexecuted([])).toEqual(['runner: no test passed, so the suite did not run']);
+    expect(unexecuted([local('I9.x', 'skipped')])).toEqual(['runner: no test passed, so the suite did not run']);
+  });
+
+  test('a skipped test beside passed ones is named', () => {
